景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

金利上昇の「質」が問われる日本
～「良い金利上昇」を実現するための4つの論点～
（2026年7月31日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年8月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

### Page 2

足許の経済・金融状況と注目ポイント
1
（注）予想はLSEG、ブルームバーグが取りまとめた予想コンセンサス
（出所）LSEG、ブルームバーグより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
2026. 10.20
9月小売売上高は実質減も10月以降の年末商戦
は堅調予想。鉱工業生産は増産基調を維持。物
価は原油高や関税の影響から消費者物価が加速、
生産者物価も大幅上昇し、インフレへの警戒強まる
米
国足
許
の
経
済
・
金
融
状
況
（
総
合
評
価
）
経済が伸び悩む一方で、インフレ圧力の強まりを確
認する結果。8月鉱工業生産は2カ月連続で減少。
貿易収支も輸出減少と輸入増加を背景に赤字に
転化。対して9月消費者物価は前月から伸び加速
欧
州
7～9月期の実質GDPは前年比＋4.5％と小幅加
速。中国政府はインフラ投資を加速させ、通年の成
⾧率目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の確保を狙う。9月の輸
出はAIブームと自動車の輸出攻勢で伸び加速
中
国
8月機械受注は市場予想を上回り、設備投資の先
行きに明るさ。鉱工業生産は確報値で下方修正さ

れ、減産幅が拡大。9月国内企業物価は前月から
減速したものの、依然として高い伸びが継続
日
本
先週（10/12～10/19）発表の主な経済指標
世界的なAI投資ブームが日本にも波及、設備投資需要が拡大。
ブームを受けて銅やレアメタルなどの鉱物需要も増加。資源開発
投資の回復は鈍く、将来の需給逼迫やインフレ圧力要因に
IMFは市場の底堅さを評価も、金融安定リスクへの警戒を維持。
ヘッジファンドの分析では、レバレッジや取引集中による相場変動
の増幅、プライムブローカーを通じた銀行部門への波及を警戒
注目点② 金融市場に潜むリスクの増幅経路注目点①AI投資拡大と資源供給制約

世界：ヘッジファンドのレバレッジ拡大が市場ショックを増幅、銀行部門への波及にも警戒
 高まるヘッジファンドの存在感と市場への影響力
― ヘッジファンドの総資産は2013年の約4兆ドルから2026年初には約13兆ドルに拡大。平時には市場の流動性や効率性
を支える一方、ストレス時にはレバレッジ解消や取引集中が価格変動を増幅させる懸念
 ヘッジファンドの損失がプライムブローカーを通じて銀行部門に波及するリスク
―IMFはヘッジファンドの運用成績悪化がプライムブローカーの収益にも悪影響を及ぼすと分析。銀行自身の健全性に加
え、ノンバンクとの相互連関を通じた金融システム全体への波及にも警鐘

―9月の実質小売売上高は前月比▲0.5％と減少。ガソリン小売が減少したほか、自動車や衣料品も低調に推移
 物価高は逆風も、2026年の年末商戦は堅調予想
― 米マスターカードは、実質小売売上高（除くガソリン・自動車）は前年比＋2.7％と、前年（同＋2.1％）から伸びが拡大する
と予想。雇用環境の改善などを背景に幅広い所得層で安定した消費がみられると指摘
10

11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持

― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み

南亞科(2408)

投資評等 目標價 
買進 $800 
前次投資建議：2026.07.13： 
「買進」 ，目標價: $710

報告日期：2026.10.13 研 究 員：王穎

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好記憶體需求緊俏有利報價走升，維持「買進」 投資評等，目標價 800 元(6 x 27(F)PER)，
潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/12 收盤價： 527.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 
611.7%，毛利率 85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利
益為 1068.9億元 ， 業外收入24.38億 ， 稅後淨利為 876.3
億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營收成
長及毛利雙雙成長受惠 平均銷售單價季增超過 六十位數
百分比，而季減中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分
比。 
➢ 展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，
且南亞科 AI相關產品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載
出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價將持續於 26Q4上
漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 
88.1%，EPS為 30.35 元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於
DRAM產能持續滿載，預期 DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產
品將持續供不應求。1C 產品預期於 27Q1 開始驗證，有
利打入 AI伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此
兆豐預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，
毛利率 83.3/86.3%，EPS 為 77.85/133.2 元。南亞科目
前位於(3.9 X 27(F) PER)，看好記憶體需求緊俏報價持續
走升， 維持「買進」投資評等，目標價  800 元(6 x 
27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 51.8%。

營 運 分 析

南亞科技為全球前四大 DRAM廠商 
南亞科技主要致力 DRAM (動態隨機存取記憶體)的研發、設計、製造與銷售，
開發自主技術，發展智慧財產權，培育關鍵技術研發團隊，落實綠色科技及
智慧製造全面提升製程效能與產能，是全球第4大的 DRAM公司。公司已成
功開發 10 奈米級 DRAM 製程技術，將可微縮至少 3 個世代，並用以發展
DDR5及 LPDDR5 等下世代產品，強化公司自主技術開發與製程轉換佈局。
第二世代 10 奈米級製程技術(1B)產品 16Gb DDR5 已完成設計與試產，於
2024 年量產。南亞科技具備完整 DRAM 產品線，可因應智慧世代的發展，
滿足多元化的產品需求。預期 1C產品於 27Q1 開始驗證。

26Q3 ASP成長帶動營收毛利雙雙上升 
26Q3 營收  1336.5 億元新台幣， QoQ+61.9%，YoY+ 611.7%，毛利率
85.7% ，營業費用 75.88 億元，營業利益為 1068.9 億元，業外收入 24.38
億，稅後淨利為 876.3 億元﹐EPS 為 25.4 元，每股淨值為 118.99元。營
收成長及毛利雙雙成長受惠平均銷售單價季增超過六十位數百分比，而季減
中個位數百分比，匯率有利低個位數百分比。

26Q4合約價持續上漲  
展望 26Q4，受惠 AI 需求續強預期 DRAM 將持續缺貨，且南亞科 AI 相關產
品已佔營收逾 20%，雖受限產能滿載出貨量將無法再提升，然而預期合約價
將持續於 26Q4 上漲 15~20%，營收及毛利率將持續創高。因此兆豐預估營
收 1556.04 億元，QoQ+16.4%，YoY+417.1%，毛利率 88.1%，EPS 為 30.35
元。

個股報告

圖三、DRAM合約價報價走勢圖

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

南亞科完成 787.2 億元私募案，攜手四大客戶搶佔 AI 供應鏈先機 
南亞科日前完成總額新台幣  787.2 億元的私募認購，吸引四大核心客戶參
與。增資後，相關投資方持股比例達 10.19%。雙方同時簽署供應協議，確
保未來能穩定供應多元 DRAM 產品組合。主要目的在於強化南亞科與客戶
的戰略合作，透過 e-SSD 與網通產品等領域，建立 AI 及雲端服務供應商
（CSP）供應鏈中的雙贏局面。私募資金將注資新廠以提升產能，進一步確
立南亞科在 AI 價值鏈中的市場地位。本次私募後，南亞科總發行股數將達 
34.5 億股。

記憶體供不應求，產能持續擴充 
目前產能為 6.5 萬片/月，產能利用率維持滿載，2026Q1~Q3 資本支出 276
億，2026 以不超過 697 億元為上限，設備佔比五成．5A新廠第一階段 35,900
片/月 於 2026~2029 資本支出約 3,466 億．新廠預計於 2027 年初開始裝機，
主要生產 1B/1C/1D製程，預期 27年底開出 20k/月，2028 年達每月投片量
達 30k/月，主要因應網通、eSSD等產品之強勁需求。

AI 帶動記憶體強勁需求，有利報價持續走升 
展望 2026~2027 年，受惠 AI 需求持續強勁三大原廠於 DRAM 產能持續滿
載，預期DDR5、DDR4、DDR3等產品將持續供不應求。1C產品預期於 27Q1
開始驗證，有利打入 AI 伺服器供應鏈，預期 AI 占比將逐季提高。因此兆豐
預估營收 4208.9/6993.1 億元，YoY+532.1/66.2%，毛利率83.3/86.3%，EPS
為 77.85/133.2 元。

記憶體持續供不應
求，報價持續走升。 
2027 新產能主要 用
於生產 1B/1C/1D 製
程。 
大型私募案確保未來
穩定供應多元 DRAM
產品組合。

syj214134-71478@esunsec.com.tw

研 究  報 告                
南亞科(2408.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
價格與⻑約重議價推升獲利，AI 需求⽀撐中⻑期成⻑  
 投資評價與建議 
受惠與記憶體供不應求持續，目前⻑約覆蓋率約50%～70%之間，合約期限從
季度到⻑達3年不等，其中，3年期合約佔總產出20%以上，有助營運能見度提
升；另隨著AI應用占比提升，有助降低記憶體景氣循環影響。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整，ASP季增超過60%： 3Q26營收季增
61.9%至1,336.5億元，年增611.7%，受惠平均售價季增60+%，其⻑約貢獻超
過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率79.5%攀升至85.7%，
營業淨利率80%，季增6.3ppts，營業費用75.88億元，費用增加主因當期及未
來的員工薪酬增加，稅後淨利876.27億元，稅後EPS 25.4元，優於預期。 
4Q26供不應求延續，預期ASP持續上揚、出貨持平：展望4Q26，公司預期市
場供不應求持續，ASP持續向上，惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，
預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，待4Q27新廠開始投產，預估
4Q26營收季增21.7%至1,626.7億元，⽑利率86.6%，稅後31.29元。 
随新廠產能開出，目標AI基礎設施逐步提升：新廠規劃1Q27裝機，因無大
幅增加產出的空間，加上庫存處於低水位，預期2027年初出貨量持平。南
亞科目前AI基礎設施相關營收占比已超過20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統管
理、eSSD以及RDIMM模組等，2030年以前目標提升至40～50%。 
 投資風險 
AI基礎建設投資放緩、客製化AI/WoW產品進度不如預期

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況  
3Q26 反應⻑期合約重新簽訂之調整 ， ASP 季增超過 60% ：3Q26 營收季增 61.9%至 1,336.5 億元 ， 年增 611.7% ，
受惠平均售價季增 60+%，其⻑約貢獻超過⼀半以上；位元出貨量季減中個位數百分比，⽑利率 79%攀升至 85.7%，
營業淨利 1,068.9 億元，營業淨利率 80%，季增 6.3ppts，營業費用 75.88 億元，費用增加主因當期及未來的員
工薪酬增加 ， 稅後淨利 876.27 億元 ， 淨利率 65.6% ， 稅後 EPS 25.4 元 ， 優於預期 。 產品結構部份 ， DDR4/LPDDR4
佔比逾 60%，DDR5 約佔 10%；此外，AI 基礎設施(含伺服器、網路、儲存及系統管理)占營收超過 20%。 
4Q26 供不應求延續，預期 ASP 持續上揚、出貨持平：展望 4Q26，公司預期市場供不應求持續，ASP 持續向上，
惟在位元出貨量，由於目前庫存處於低檔，預期未來數個季度的位元出貨量將保持平穩，直到 4Q27 新廠開始投
產，預估 4Q26 營收季增 21.7%至 1,626.7 億元，⽑利率 86.6%，稅後 31.29 元。此外，就供需市況部份，隨著 AI
運算主導 DRAM 需求成⻑ ， 延伸至網路 、 儲存及系統管理等應用 ， 推升高容量 、 高頻寬及客製化記憶體架構需求 ，
而傳統應用因受限於供給不足加上報價調漲，轉而降低規格、延⻑⽣命週期來因應；另供給則因新廠建置、設備
交期、製程導入，均需要較⻑的前置時間，預期供不應求狀況在 2027 年至 2028 年前難以解決。 
随新廠產能開出，目標 AI 基礎設施應用占比逐步提升：產能規劃方面，新廠規劃 1Q27 裝機，因無大幅增加產出
的空間，再加上庫存消耗後，正處低水位，預期 2027 年初出貨量持平，隨著 4Q27 新廠開始投產後有望逐步提
升，目標 2028 年達每月投片量３萬片，2030 年年底達到每月投片量５萬片。此外，第三代 10 奈米級製程(1C)
預計 1Q27 開始客戶驗證，並同步開發第四代(1D)、第五代(1E)製程及 EUV，均按計畫推進。另規劃於屏東科學
園區建置 3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 年年底前導入矽穿孔(TSV)、晶圓堆疊等 3D IC 先進技術，未來擴建
皆以客戶⻑期合約的 AI 專案為目標。南亞科目前 AI 基礎設施相關營收占比已超過 20%，含蓋通訊、儲存、系統
管理、eSSD 以及 RDIMM 模組等，2030 年以前目標提升至 40～50%；此外，目前⻑約覆蓋率約 50%～70%之
間，合約期限從季度到⻑達 3 年不等，其中，3 年期合約佔總產出 20%以上，有助營運能見度提升；另與客戶合
作 AI 相關晶圓對晶圓技術 ASIC 專案，應用範圍涵蓋雲端 AI 運算、邊緣運算及物理 AI，已有營收貢獻，雖然目
前規模不大，後續業務發展值得關注。預估 2027 年營收 7,378.76 億元，⽑利率 87.1%，稅後 EPS 141.9 元，維
持「買進」之投資建議，目標價 710 元(3.8 倍 PBR*2027BVPS(F))。

### Page 2

Taiwan Electronics & Semiconductors
12 October 2026 Citi Research
2
PCB and substrate- 3Q26 was a strong quarter for substrate and CCL sectors, with
demand continuously piling up. Unimicron posted +23% QoQ sales growth in 3Q26,
beating Citi/consensus forecasts by 4%/6%. We expect 4Q26E ABF sales
momentum to be even stronger vs 3Q considering all the AI chips ramping up
together. CCL names also saw strong momentum across the board thanks to
enhanced product mix and benefit from price hike in 2Q26. We expect CCL sector to

see another round of price hikes from Nov onwards. For PCB, GCE’s Sept sales were
impacted by flooding in Thailand, which led to logistics disruption, while Tripod
posted record monthly sales backed by memory demand and early pull-in before
China Golden Week. With TPCA Show coming next week, we think
substrate/CCL/PCB sectors might see some event catalysts in the near term.
However, from a fundamental perspective, we still prefer substrate
(Unimicron>NYPCB>Kinsus) > CCL (EMC> TUC) > PCB.

PC/NB Supply chain - For NB ODMs, overall NB shipments see MoM increase in Sep
(+32% MoM) due to the quarter end inventory build; combined 3Q NB shipments
were down 17% QoQ due to  the early pull-in in 1H. We forecast 4Q26E NB shipments
to further decline -7% QoQ/-28% YoY given the muted demand on rising retails
prices and the increasing constraint in components procurement, While the price
hike effect in the NB products should partially offset shipment decline, we expect

increasing margins dilution impact to the NB ODM supply chains.
For lens components - Largan reported relatively muted sales results in Sep at
NT$5,779m (+15% MoM/-7% YoY); combined 3Q revenues are below market
estimates while Genius’ 3Q revenues were slightly better than expectation. Despite
the near-term sales performance, we see more upside opportunity coming from
their new development in optical business in the longer term. We expect Genius’
project with a leading US company on optical connector and ferrule to start

shipment in 1H27. For Largan, we expect it to benefit most from FA/FAU for CPO
market. We see its current engagement with the leading US client progressing
smoothly, as evidenced by its continued new land expansion for future capacity
build-out (see our recent report).
Thermal – Sep-26 sales as reported by thermal players (AVC & Auras) were up from
Aug at +6% MoM/+42% YoY. While AVC delivered another record month thanks to
solid ASIC demand and initial Vera Rubin shipments, Auras experienced MoM

decline due to client deferring pull-ins and Thailand flooding. Going forward, we
preliminarily expect both companies to see sequential revenue growth in 4Q26E,
driven by the AWS Trainium transition from air to liquid cooling, ramp-up of the TPU
and Vera Rubin projects.
Power – Delta reported MoM revenue recovery in Sep-26 (+1% MoM/+14% YoY), with
power electronics down 4% MoM. 3Q26 revenues were up 8% QoQ but below our
and Street’s expectation. We expect Delta to regain momentum with double-digit

QoQ growth in 4Q26E as ±400V enters the initial transition period, while in the long
term, HVDC is still progressing gradually while Delta maintains its technology
leadership. For BBU, we see Sep sales showing mixed momentum yet resilient BBU
demand remains intact. Dynapack’s Sep-26 sales declined 34% y/y and 54% m/m
to NT$776mn, reflecting 1) weaker PC shipments amid quarter-end inventory
adjustments and 2) deferred shipments of one BBU product, broadly consistent with

the near-term weakness flagged in our Aug-26 sales note. We expect a meaningful
sales rebound from Oct helped by shipments of new higher-power BBU products.
Meanwhile, AES maintained solid momentum with Sep-26 sales rising 25% y/y (+1%
m/m), suggesting resilient underlying BBU demand. Similarly, STL (4931.TWO; not
covered) delivered robust 176% y/y growth, further reinforcing our positive view on
the sector. We retain our constructive stance on BBU names, supported by

sustained demand and upcoming product ramps
Passive components - Yageo reported trended up monthly sales in Sep-26, as
demand in Europe and US recovered after the summer holiday, on top of sustained
AI demand. 3Q26 revenues were slightly above cons forecasts. Current quarter top
line still beats mgmt. 's "moderate growth" guidance. We believe that growth was
again broad-based across commodity and specialty products, and driven by both
volume and price. We expect a more visible ASP uplift in 4Q26E as specialty and

contract-based business could slow vs the spot and distributor channels.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

Key Takeaways from Company Meetings

Key Takeaways from Applied Materials Meeting 
Participants: Kevin Moraes, VP , Strategy and Marketing; Sree Kesapragada, VP , 
Advanced Packaging; Mike Sullivan, Head of Investor Relations 
Investor questions mainly focused on clarifications tied to the company’s investor event 
and financial targets. On revenue and capital intensity, management noted that 
semiconductor industry pricing is a key variable that it cannot control - but has high 
confidence in its framework tied to increasing WFE intensity per node - and these 
increases have already been programmatically locked in by customers today. On 
Applied’s target of 200bps of market share gain, management noted the complex set of 
mix effects involved in the calculation, but noted the substantial impact from China 
export control exclusions. Relative to the company’s newly articulated gross margin 
target of 54% by 2030, Applied believes there is the potential for upside to this target - 
but pointed out that the vast majority of its costs are variable in nature. On its optical 
tool portfolio, Applied believes that it can leverage its expertise and market position in 
e-beam technology to expand into this adjacent $9bn market. On AGS, management has 
high confidence in its outlook for mid-teens long term revenue growth - but noted that 
its services attach rate is higher on its newer tools relative to its installed fleet. 
Key Takeaways from Lam Research Meeting 
Participants: Ram Ganesh - VP of Investor Relations 
Consistent with prior commentary, Lam expects 8-10 new fab projects to come online in 
2027 - and currently expects product growth to be driven by DRAM, followed by 
advanced foundry/logic, followed by NAND flash. The company has not specified a 
timeframe for achieving its new long-term gross margin target of ~55%. However, we 
expect this to be achieved over several years, and see it tied more to product mix 
(particularly with new products such as Akara) and overseas operational manufacturing 
efficiencies rather than like-for-like pricing. Management remains constructive on the 
company’s competitive positioning in Logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging - 
particularly as etch and deposition intensity continues to grow with a variety of new 
device structures coming into the mix - including hard masks, surface prep, TSVs, and 
other materials engineering steps. 
Key Takeaways from Teradyne Meeting 
Participants: Roy Chorev - VP & GM of Compute Test Division; Amy McAndrews - VP of 
Investor Relations 
Teradyne announced a strategic partnership with and investment in FormFactor at 
SEMICON West. Teradyne remains committed to an open ecosystem of probe card 
vendors, but believes Form’s strong position in silicon photonics warrants a closer 
relationship that enables Teradyne to commit additional engineering platform resources. 
Regarding its XPU business, Teradyne continues to expect to gain merchant GPU share in 
the long run - and noted that Rubin test times are nearly 2X Blackwell given the near 
doubling in transistor count. Teradyne is also pursuing the tester opportunity at Intel, 
which is focused on increasing its exposure to external vendors (currently using an 
14 October 2026   2
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 3

internal solution) beginning with its foundry business, where Teradyne sees a
medium-term opportunity. In its memory business, Teradyne expects minimal impact
from customer de-specing of HBM as its demand is tied primarily to unit volume rather
than stack height. In co-packaged optics, Teradyne continues to see a very strong
product ramp opportunity, although given the early stages of development, there are
likely to be significant shifts between testing intensity at different insertions during the

manufacturing process.
Key Takeaways from Camtek Investor Meeting
Participants: Lior Aviram - Executive Chairman; Rafi Amit - CEO; Ramy Langer -
President & COO; Moshe Eisenberg - CFO; Eyal Segev - Marketing Director
We attended Camtek’s Investor Meeting at SEMICON. Our key takeaways are as follows:
Long-term financial targets: In conjunction with its investor meeting, Camtek n
pre-announced 3Q26 revenue of $160 mn –$161 mn (above its prior guidance),

guided to ~$180 mn for 4Q26, and provided an initial 2027 revenue outlook of $800
mn based on orders received to date. Management laid out a long-term revenue
target of $1.2 billion in about three years, with a gross margin target of 55%-56%
and operating margin target of 34%-36%.
Advanced packaging remains the key growth driver: Camtek stressed that n
advanced packaging remains its core business, accounting for ~80% of recent
orders, and expects advanced packaging wafer growth to run at about twice the

broader industry rate through 2031 (13% vs. 7% CAGR). Within advanced packaging,
the company expects the high-end of the market (which accounts for the vast
majority of its business) to grow at a ~24% CAGR through 2031, driven by AI, silicon
photonics, denser interconnects, and increasingly complex inspection and metrology
requirements.
Technology and product roadmap centered on inspection and specialized n
metrology: Camtek’s roadmap remains centered on its margin-accretive Hawk and

Eagle products for inspection (~85% of its 2026 mix), as well as its metrology
platforms. Separately, management also noted it is embedding AI into detection and
classification and plans to launch AI-enabled software in 2027 (which it attributed to
its Visual Layer acquisition).
M&A strategy focused on capability acquisition and strategic expansion: Camtek n
noted that it is pursuing both smaller acquisitions that add technology and/or talent
to its core business, as well as larger strategic transactions that broaden its offering,

while emphasizing that its $1.2 bn revenue target and financial model do not assume
meaningful M&A activity.
Key Takeaways from Onto Innovation Meeting
Participants: Brian Roberts - CFO; Sidney Ho - VP of Investor Relations
Onto remains very constructive on the demand outlook through 2H26 and heading into
2027 across a range of demand drivers - with growth likely to accelerate next year given
the growth of incremental market opportunities. In HBM, Onto believes its business is

likely to be minimally impacted by any “de-spec” activity by XPU vendors, as its tool
14 October 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

demand is far more driven by unit volume than stack height. The company continues to
see CoWoS as a key demand driver that is increasingly driven by OSAT partners rather
than TSMC; Onto sees EMIB-T as a significant opportunity that promises to be material in
2028. From a competitive standpoint, Onto remains highly confident in its market share
position, including at TSMC - and sees no imminent impact from potential product
launches by competitors. On margins, management remains confident in its target of

350bps of gross margin expansion, which it believes can be achieved solely by product
mix and efficiencies in its manufacturing operations. The company believes it has room
to grow revenue substantially from projected levels, and does not see itself as capacity
constrained.
Key Takeaways from MKS Instruments Meeting
Participants: John Lee - President & CEO; Paretosh Misra - VP of Investor Relations
MKS believes the industry is in the early-to-middle stages of a substantial cyclical

industry upturn driven by AI and datacenter demand. The company is currently
preparing for a potential WFE spending envelope of up to $300bn in 2028, and
potentially higher over time. In the near-term, management expects the company to
potentially outgrow its customers (2Q26 was the first time the company delivered Y/Y
Semiconductor revenue growth in excess of them) given supply chain inventory effects.
MKS believes it has enough capacity to support WFE of ~$250bn with its Penang facility

(with a surge capacity of another 20%), but may need to expand capacity further if
these forecasts come to fruition. Importantly, the company does not believe it is
constraining its customers’ growth plans today. MKS has not taken significant pricing
actions, but could do so selectively in order to capture the value it delivers. Management
believes its product portfolio is well positioned, but would consider a possible
divestiture of its specialty industrial product lines at an attractive valuation.

### Page 5

Valuation/Risks

AMAT (Buy, on CL): Our 12-month price target of $670 is based on a 32X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS estimate of $21.00. Key downside risks include: (1) 
incremental export restrictions; (2) local China-based supplier share gains. 
LRCX (Buy): Our price target is $380, which is based on a 35X multiple applied to our 
normalized EPS forecast of $10.75. Key downside risks: (1) softer DRAM/HBM demand; 
(2) delays in planned clean room and fab expansions; (3) lower-than-expected content 
gains from advanced packaging and 3D architectures. 
TER (Buy): Our 12-month price target is $465, which is based on a 37X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS forecast of $12.50. Key downside risks: (1) delays in the 
revenue ramp with a merchant GPU customer, (2) significant quarterly volatility due to 
the lumpy nature of large AI-related infrastructure projects, (3) slower-than-expected 
recovery in Mobile. 
ONTO (Buy): Our12-month price target is $400, which is based on 32X our normalized 
EPS estimate of $12.50. Our target P/E is in line with peer multiples, and it is on the 
higher end of ONTO’s historical median, given recent momentum around HPC. Key 
upside/downside risks: (1) additional volume purchase agreements and accelerated 
customer adoption of Dragonfly G5; (2) shift towards OSATs or domestic Chinese 
manufacturers; (3) competitor launching a rival tool. 
CAMT (Neutral): Our 12-m price target of $160 is based on a 38X P/E multiple applied 
to our normalized EPS of $4.20. Key upside/downside risks: (1) notable push-outs in 
chiplet architectures; (2) failure to pass on higher pricing; (3) better-than-expected 
growth in HPC-driven revenue in the medium-term. 
MKSI (Sell): Our 12-month price target is $270, which is based on a 20X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS of $13.50. Key upside risks: (1) rebound in the NAND 
market from an equipment ordering perspective; (2) greater realization of synergies 
from the Atotech acquisition; (3) any material pickup in momentum for the company’s 
Semiconductor business due to broader WFE trends.

### Page 6

Wiwynn (6669.TW)
19 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Wiwynn
Company description
Established in April 2012 and listed in March 2019, Wiwynn is one of the
leading cloud infrastructure providers focusing on servers, storage, and rack
solutions for global CSPs' data centers. Key customers include Microsoft,
Meta Platforms, and Amazon, which together accounted for >90% of sales
with tier-2 CSPs accounting for single-digit percentage of sales. Wiwynn
used to be running at an asset-light business model but has expanded

capacity for server motherboard manufacturing and rack final assembly on its
own in recent years, increasing in-house supply ratio.
Investment strategy
We rate Wiwynn as Buy. We expect the secular growth in global data center
and cloud-based IP traffic to act as a key driver for Wiwynn in years to come.
We expect Wiwynn to benefit from data center growth amid rising ODM
Direct/white-box server adoption. With order momentum having started to
recover since 4Q23, we see Wiwynn's growth being backed by continuous

rollouts of data centers from existing customers and new project wins from
Amazon.
Valuation
Our target price for Wiwynn is NT$3,200. We value Wiwynn based on PE as
we believe the company’s earnings performance and industry dynamics will
be the key focus areas for the market. Our TP is based on 16x 2027E EPS,
which is at the top end of the historical trading range since listing and above
the average PE multiple of the peer group. We believe our target multiple is

justified by the company’s 100% exposure to data centers, rising scale, and
robust earnings growth, as well as stabilizing ROE.
Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Wiwynn’s shares to trade below our
target price include: 1) Potential volatility in capex deployment; 2) Component
price hike; 3) Peer competition; and 4) FX volatility.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響 ， 加上新產
品良率及綠電採購成本壓力 ， 預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對
保守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司
發揮精密光學設計與製造優勢；另一方面， FAU 預計於 2H27
進入量產，隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來
源。 考量大立光具備高精度光學製造、 微米級對位技術及自動化
設備開發能力， 技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢， 正面看待公
司中長期發展。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入
為 706.92 億元，Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，
營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，
Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。研究部考量儘管 2026 年消費
性電子需求疲弱，且 CPO 產品短期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度
優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，2027 年營運有望重回成長
軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目標價訂為 7,800 元，投
資建議為買進。

餘也包含平板、筆記型電腦、車用及其他消費性電子產品，在全球
光學鏡頭市占率約佔 30%。其總部位於台中市南屯區精密機械園
區，共有十座廠區及兩座興建中廠區。前十大股東持股合計
38.06%。客戶為 APPLE、華為、三星、小米、OPPO、VIVO 等手

⚫ 產業概況 
記憶體成本飆升壓抑出貨，智慧型手機市場進入量縮價增 
根據研調機構數據 ，2026 年全球智慧型手機市場受記憶體供應短缺
及成本大幅上漲影響 ， 出貨量預估將Y-16.7%至 10.5 億支 ， 較前一
季預估的 Y-13.9%再度下修，並創下歷年最大年度衰退幅度，其中
2H26 出貨量更預估 Y-27.2%。 由於記憶體成本年增超過300%，品
牌廠難以自行吸收成本，陸續透過漲價及調整產品組合因應，帶動
2026 年智慧型手機平均售價 Y+27.6%至 581 美元，全球市場產值
反而 Y+6.3%至 6,130 億美元，呈現「量縮價增」格局。產品結構
方面，低階市場受到衝擊最為明顯，尤其100 美元以下機種因原先
毛利空間有限，品牌廠陸續縮減產品線，2Q26 該價位出貨量已年
減近 60%；相較之下，高階機種因消費者價格敏感度較低，且成熟
市場具分期付款等機制支撐，需求相對具韌性，帶動整體市場加速
朝高階化發展。 
展望未來，記憶體供需緊張預期至少延續至 2028 年，全球市場短

低階機種需求仍將承受較大壓力，而美國、英國等成熟市場則因高
階機種占比較高，受漲價影響相對有限。品牌競爭方面， Apple、

及高階產品布局，在產業成本結構上升下相對有利，市場集中度可
望進一步提高。

⚫ 未來展望 
拉貨動能逐步趨緩，良率改善有望緩解毛利率壓力 
展望 4Q26，預期 10 月拉貨量與 9 月持平，11 月起則隨新機拉貨
高峰結束而逐步下滑 ， 儘管客戶1H27 新產品已自 10 月開始拉貨，
仍難以完全彌補既有訂單下修影響，加上目前客戶需求能見度偏
短，第四季營收動能仍具不確定性。毛利率方面，儘管新機生產良
率逐步改善 ， 然而綠電採購成本持續增加 ， 相關成本最快須至2028
年才能反映於報價，預期短期毛利率改善幅度仍將受到限制。綜合
以上 ， 研究部預估大立光4Q26 營業收入為 155.21 億元 ，Y-9.86%、
Q-0.83%，毛利率 46.65%，Y-1.32ppts、Q+4.44ppts，營業利益
率 35.29%，Y-1.46ppts、Q+5.04ppts，稅後純益 54.91 億元，
Y-18.28%，EPS 為 41.98 元。綜合以上，研究部預估大立光 2026
年營業收入為 603.80 億元 ，Y-1.26%， 毛利率46.84%，Y-3.59ppts，
營 業 利 益 率 35.06% ，Y-3.47ppts ， 稅 後 純 益 214.67 億元，
Y+0.90%，EPS 為 164.12 元。

手機主鏡頭規格持續升級，高階光學產品支撐營運動能 
展望 2027 年，智慧型手機主鏡頭將有規格升級，帶動高階鏡頭設
計提升，目前相關產品已完成技術準備並持續送樣，有望隨新機導
入逐步貢獻營收。潛望式鏡頭方面，下一波較大幅度規格升級預計
落在 2028 年，目前已進入試產階段，2027 年則以既有產品出貨為
主。獲利方面，因綠電採購成本持續增加，短期毛利率改善仍將取
決於新產品良率及產品組合變化。

FA 預計 2H27 量產，精密對位與整合能力開啟光通訊新成長動能 
光通方面，FA 產品已通過客戶認證，FAU 則持續進行送樣及整合
驗證 ，FAU 預計 2027 年年中完成產線建置 ，2H27 正式進入量產。
初期以單排 FA 及 Grating Couplin 技術為主，並朝 FAU 與 Carrier
整合出貨發展 ， 提高單一產品附加價值 ， 多排FA 及 Edge Coupling
則持續開發 ， 預計2028 年後逐步導入 。 公司憑藉微米級精密對位、
自製自動化設備及檢測能力 ， 可望在高技術門檻的FAU 市場建立競
爭優勢。預期 2027 年光通營收貢獻仍處於起步階段，隨後續產能
與良率提升，有望成為中長期成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估大
立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元 ，Y+17.08%， 毛利率51.37%，
Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24
億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08 元。

⚫ 結論與建議 
大立光短期營運受智慧型手機新機拉貨動能趨緩影響，加上新產品
良率及綠電採購成本壓力，預期第四季營收及獲利表現仍相對保
守。展望 2027 年，手機主鏡頭持續朝高規格升級，有利公司發揮
精密光學設計與製造優勢 ； 另一方面 ，FAU 預計於 2H27 進入量產 ，
隨 CPO 技術逐步發展，有望成為中長期新增營收來源。考量大立
光具備高精度光學製造、微米級對位技術及自動化設備開發能力，
技術門檻高且具備跨領域應用優勢，正面看待公司中長期發展。綜
合以上，研究部預估大立光 2027 年營業收入為 706.92 億元，
Y+17.08%，毛利率 51.37%，Y+4.53ppts，營業利益率 40.04%，
Y+4.98ppts，稅後純益 268.24 億元，Y+24.96%，EPS 為 205.08
元。研究部考量儘管2026 年消費性電子需求疲弱 ，且CPO 產品短
期營收貢獻仍有限，然而進度優於先前預期，隨新產品逐步放量，
2027 年營運有望重回成長軌道，給予大立光 38 倍本益比評價，目
標價訂為 7,800 元，投資建議為買進。

Document 210: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

작성자(최관순)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

Document 263: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CAPITAL FLOWS BANKING
*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 26-30
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 24 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.5% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 0.7%, both
posting second straight weekly losses. The Nasdaq’s 2.9% slide on
Thursday was its biggest one-day drop since June, as concerns flared
over the cost and availability of financial capital for AI investment. The
price of WTI crude topped $100 a barrel amid US-Iran conflict concerns,
while the 10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.0%.
The FOMC meeting (Wed) is the focus this week, alongside the release of

Q3 GDP and September's PCED inflation report (Thu). San Francisco Fed
President Mary Daly and St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem speak
on Friday. A heavy week of Q3 earnings reports rounds out a packed
calendar. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC. The FOMC’s two-day meeting concludes Wednesday, followed
by Fed Chair Kevin Warsh’s press conference. The CME FedWatch Tool
puts the odds of a hike this week at 9% and the odds of one in December
at 84%. Federal funds futures imply 2.7 rate hikes over the next 12

months and 1.4 over the next six (chart). We believe the committee should
hike on Wednesday but won't because the midterm elections are so close.

### Page 4

(2) GDP & PCED. The first estimate of Q3 GDP (Thu) follows Q2’s third
estimate of 2.2% saar, with the GDP price index up 6.1%. The Atlanta
Fed’s GDPNow model estimates Q3 growth at 3.5% as of October 20, led
by a 17.8% jump in business equipment spending (chart).
September’s core PCED (Thu) follows August’s 3.0% y/y. That was well
below September’s 4.6% pace of core PPI final demand for personal
consumption and above the core CPI’s 2.6% (chart). The Cleveland Fed’s

Inflation Nowcasting model projects September’s core and headline PCED
at 3.1% and 3.6% y/y, up from August’s 3.0% and 3.4%. (The model’s
m/m rates are 0.28% and 0.47%.)
CPI computer software and accessories rose 28.4% y/y in September and
PPI electronic components 26.8%, while PCED computers and peripheral

### Page 5

equipment rose just 8.4% in August. September’s PCED will show
whether that gap starts to close.
(3) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a drop in the four-week average to 18,500 for the week
ended October 3, down from 26,000 the prior week (chart). That’s still
more than double July’s low of 8,250, pointing to a solid October payrolls
report.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 194,000, below expectations

and near the lowest level since the late 1960s. The four-week average
stands at 197,800 (chart). Continuing claims rose to 1,728,000 for the
week ended October 9, up from 1,710,000, while their four-week average
edged down to 1,708,000.

### Page 6

(4) Regional business surveys. The Richmond Fed’s manufacturing
index (Tue) follows September’s -2 reading. The regional M-PMI business
activity index slipped to 13.4 in September from August’s 18.7, while the
national M-PMI eased to 54.5 from 54.6 (chart).
(5) Earnings. The earnings reporting season heats up this week, with
36% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Boeing, Seagate, and
Corning report on Tuesday. Alphabet, Microsoft, SK Hynix, and GE

Vernova follow on Wednesday. Samsung, Intel, Eli Lilly, SanDisk, and
Amazon report on Thursday. Exxon Mobil reports on Friday.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 24.1% y/y, slightly up from last week (chart). Q4
estimates have also risen.

### Page 7

(6) Global central banks. Both the Reserve Bank of New Zealand and
the Bank of Canada (Wed) are expected to hike by 25bps. The markets
are pricing in 88% odds that the RBNZ will lift its official cash rate to
3.00% and an 85% chance that the BOC will raise its policy rate to 2.50%.
The European Central Bank (Thu) and the Bank of Japan (Fri) are set to
hold their policy rates at 2.50% and 1.25%, respectively (chart).
December is the focus for the latter pair. The markets are pricing in 75%

odds of an ECB hike in December and a 74% probability of a BOJ hike.
Guidance from ECB President Christine Lagarde and BOJ Governor
Kazuo Ueda will show whether those odds hold.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank of Thailand (BOT) (October 28)

We expect the BOT to maintain its policy 
rate at 1.00% at its 28 October meeting, 
extending its pause for a fourth 
consecutive decision as it balances 
inflationary pressures against uneven 
and subdued economic growth. Our view 
is aligned with fixed market income 
pricing. Headline inflation rose anew to 
2.8% yoy in September 2026, driven by 
higher energy, food, and core price 
increases, but remained within the upper 
end of the central bank’s 1 -3% target 
range. As the BOT is still likely to look 
through what it views as temporary, 
supply-driven inflationary pressu res, it 
will likely refrain from tightening policy, 
and keep interest rates accommodative 
to support the soft and uneven growth 
cycle.

European Central Bank (October 29)

We expect the ECB to extend its pause, 
keeping the deposit facility rate at 2.5%. 
Market participants will instead focus on 
the central bank’s assessment of rising 
French and Italian bond yields and 
widening spreads versus Bunds. While 
we do not expect dir ect references to 
individual countries, the ECB is likely to 
signal that recent developments in 
France reflect the country’s weaker 
fiscal and debt profile rather than a 
broader systemic concern. Policymakers 
will seek to differentiate between a 
localised bond sell -off and a Eurozone -
wide market disruption, justifying their 
decision to monitor developments rather 
than implement specific measures at this 
juncture. Meanwhile, inflation continues 
to rise, with little relief expected in 
upcoming readings given elevated 
global fuel prices. 
Bank of Japan (October 30)

The Bank of Japan is expected to keep 
the overnight call rate unchanged at 1.25% 
at this meeting, pausing after the 25bp 
hike in September. The BOJ is likely to 
assess the impact of the previous rate 
hike before taking further action. The 
recent stabilisat ion of the yen also 
reduces the urgency of another hike at 
this meeting.

The tightening bias should remain intact. 
Growth and inflation remain broadly on 
track, leaving room for the BOJ to revise 
up its FY2026 –FY2027 GDP and CPI 
forecasts. Wage growth has also 
remained strong, with Rengo reportedly 
targeting wage increases of 6% in 2027, 
reinforcing the BOJ’s confidence that 
underlying inflation can be sustained 
around its 2% target. We continue to 
expect the policy rate to rise to 1.50% in 
December 2026 or January 2027, 
followed by a further increase to 1.75% 
by 2Q 2027.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 53.0% yoy in August to 55.0% in 
September, in line with the pickup in 
China’s export growth from 25.0% to 
36.3%. As a key re-export hub for China,

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 23, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Hong Kong should benefit from robust 
AI-related electronics trade, with 
electrical machinery, equipment and 
parts exports to Hong Kong accounting 
for 20.6% of China’s total exports. 
Import growth is also expected to remain 
firm, supported by resilient expo rt 
orders and gradually improving 
investment sentiment, sustaining 
demand for intermediate and capital 
goods.

Singapore

Singapore’s industrial production (IP) 
likely remained in expansion for a 13th 
consecutive month in September 2026, 
although we expect growth to moderate 
to 7.6% yoy from August’s strong 15.4% 
yoy pace. IP growth continued to be 
underpinned by the electronics and 
precision engineering clusters, which 
has been key drivers of Singapore’s 
manufacturing strength, reflecting 
sustained demand for artificial 
intelligence-related hardware. However, 
performance across clusters remained 
uneven, with chemicals and biomedical 
manufacturing likely contracting, and 
tempering overall gains.

South Korea

The preliminary estimate for 3Q26 GDP 
is likely to show growth remaining solid 
at 3.2% yoy , slightly below 3.7% in 2Q 
and 3.8% in 1Q. Nominal GDP growth is 
likely to remain in double digits, 
significantly outpacing real GDP growth, 
thanks to improved terms of trade amid 
rising memory export prices. By 
expenditure, exports picked up in 3Q in 
both nominal and real terms, supported 
by stronger semiconductor shipments. 
Investment also appeared robust, as 
reflected in the equipment investment 
estimation index and machinery order 
index. Private consumption, however, 
appeared to weaken amid the sharp 
KOSPI correction since July following  
the introduction of leveraged ETF 
regulations. Our full -year GDP growth 
forecasts of 3.5% for 2026 and 2.4% for 
2027 remain on track.

Taiwan

The preliminary estimate for 3Q GDP is 
likely to show growth remaining strong 
at 12 –13% yoy , close to 12.9% in 2Q, 
albeit below the peak of 15.4% in 1Q. 
Exports maintained strong double -digit 
growth in 3Q (44.6% yoy  in nominal 
terms and 21.1% in real terms), supported 
by robust shipments of semiconductors, 
servers and other AI -related products. 
Machinery investment also sustained 
strong double-digit growth, underpinned 
by higher capacity utilisation in the ICT 
sector and a resulting increase in capital 
goods imports. Private consumption 
appeared to have strengthened further, 
supported by stock market gains, 
improving wage growth, and the 
associated wealth and income effects. 
Construction investment remained the 
weak s pot, weighed down by elevated 
inventories in the new-home market and 
the ongoing need for inventory digestion. 
We maintain our 2026 and 2027 GDP 
growth forecasts at 11.6% and 5.6%, 
respectively, with risks tilted to the 
upside.

Subject: What Will Warsh Do?
Date:    5 hours ago
What Will Warsh Do?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
What Will Warsh Do?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 20 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Olav Ahrens Røtne / Unsplash

I. The Fed: To Hike or Not To Hike?
The Federal Reserve announces its next interest-rate decision on
Wednesday, October 28. According to CME FedWatch, the federal funds
futures market assigns an 84% probability to a pause. The market expects
a 25bps hike in December and roughly 50bps of tightening over the next
six months (chart).

### Page 2

The two-year Treasury yield remains roughly 90bps above the federal
funds rate, consistent with expectations for further tightening (chart).
Why are markets pricing in such a low probability of an October hike
despite September's hot CPI and PPI reports? The explanation may lie
more in Fed commentary than in the economic data:
(1) Fed officials favor patience. New York Fed President John Williams
and Vice Chair Philip Jefferson, who alongside Chair Kevin Warsh form

the influential troika, have signaled little urgency to hike in October.
Governor Christopher Waller suggested that hikes need not occur at
consecutive meetings, while Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle Bowman
also favors patience. Together, their forward guidance has steered the
markets toward pricing in an October pause. Even Warsh's refusal to

### Page 3

provide guidance at the IMF-World Bank Annual Meetings in Bangkok on
October 16 was interpreted as supportive of a pause.
(2) Warsh's three problems. The markets remain heavily influenced by
the very forward guidance that Warsh wants to abandon. This creates
three challenges: First, other Fed officials have continued to provide
guidance despite Warsh’s preference to move away from it. Second, that
guidance influences bond yields, making it harder for Warsh to get a clean

signal from the bond market. Third, that risks boxing him into policy
decisions that he may oppose, as he could be reluctant to surprise the
markets.
(3) The solution. We suspect Warsh would prefer an October hike.
Following the FOMC's September 16 decision to raise the federal funds
rate by 25bps, Warsh said he would be “hard pressed to describe broad
financial conditions as restrictive,” characterizing the hike as removing a
dose of accommodation.

Raising rates in October, even if the markets aren't fully pricing in such a
move, could help Warsh address his three problems. It would signal that
forward guidance from other Fed officials, even influential members of the
traditional troika, may no longer carry the weight it once did. That could
encourage investors to place less weight on Fed commentary, giving
Warsh a clearer signal from the bond market about the appropriate path of
monetary policy. In turn, it would reduce the risk of getting boxed in by

market expectations shaped by forward guidance that Warsh does not
necessarily agree with.
(4) Our call. We estimate the neutral federal funds rate at 4.25%–4.50%
and believe the Fed should return to that range, reversing last year's three
25bps insurance cuts. Those cuts were intended to protect against labor-
market weakness (that we considered overblown) and were made at a
time when the Fed was too complacent about inflation.
We believe that Warsh should try to persuade his colleagues to hike next

week. Then again, he might not do so because the midterm elections are
too close.
II. US Economy

### Page 4

The latest US economic data suggest that monetary policy remains
accommodative, exerting little restraint on economic activity or inflation:
(1) Consumer spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 9.7% y/y
during the week of October 16. The four-week average climbed 9.1%, its
fastest pace since early July and well above the 2025 average of 5.8%
(chart).
(2) Labor market. The latest weekly ADP data show private-sector
employment rising by an average of 18,500 jobs per week over the four

weeks through October 3, implying roughly 74,000 jobs per month (chart).
This suggests that the labor market remains in good shape, supporting
continued brisk consumer spending.

### Page 5

(3) Import prices. September's data on import prices point to persistent
inflation pressures. The import price index surged 8.1% y/y, the fastest
pace since July 2022 (chart). The index excluding petroleum imports rose
6.0%.
Import prices from the newly industrialized Asian countries surged 12.5%
y/y in September, mostly reflecting booming AI-related demand for
semiconductors from South Korea and Taiwan (chart). That suggests the
AI buildout will remain inflationary for now, before AI brings the fruits of

disinflationary productivity growth.
(4) GDP. The Atlanta Fed's GDPNow model estimates Q3 real GDP
growth at 3.5% (saar). Real final sales to private domestic purchasers, a
key gauge of underlying demand, is projected to rise 4.7%, a pace last
matched in Q1-2023. Real consumer spending is expected to grow 3.8%,

### Page 6

clause.

2026年 10月 22日 全9 頁

「一度出せば足りる」統計へ 
公的統計のデジタル化を問う②－負担軽減の「実装」

政策調査部 主任研究員 溝端 幹雄

[要約] 
 前編で示した公的統計のデジタル化による負担軽減の効果を安定的に得るには、「制
度・法」「標準化」「府省の縦割り」「民間データの代表性・継続性」「品質保証」「コス
トとメリット還元」「機密・信頼」「人材・予算」という課題を、効果の段階（電子化
→削減→代替）に応じて計画的に手当てする必要がある。 
 これらの課題の背景には、各府省が所管分野の統計を作る分散型統計機構の下で、標
準化、データ連携、重複報告の解消を府省横断で進めることの難しさがある。現行の
第 IV 期の公的統計基本計画（以下、基本計画）が終了する 2027 年度を見据え、次期
の第 V 期基本計画に向けては、統計委員会や総務省の調整機能、各府省の実施体制を
含むガバナンス改革が問われる。 
 「機密・信頼」という主要な懸念は、制度だけでなく技術によっても軽減できる。秘密
計算・差分プライバシー・合成データ等のプライバシー強化技術（PETs ）については、
国連が公的統計向けのガイドを整備し、各国統計機関で実証や導入検討が進められて
いる。民間データの継続性リスクに対しては、提供のインセンティブ設計に加え、デ
ータアクセスや相互運用性のルールを定める欧州データ法・データスペース等の枠組
みも参考となる。ただし、欧州データ法に基づく公的機関の民間データ取得は、例外
的な必要性がある場合等に限定される。 
 第V期基本計画には、①once-only原則の明文化、②行政記録・民間データ利活用の制
度整備、③府省共通の報告基盤、④企業へのメリット還元、⑤ガバナンス・人材・予
算を柱に据え、費用便益と工程表を伴って位置づけるべきである。企業の報告者負担
の軽減を、公的統計の持続可能性確保の中核に据える好機である。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

実現に向けた「課題」 
前編 1では、報告者負担を軽減するデジタル化の効果を、① 調査の電子化、② 行政記録情
報・企業保有データの連携による調査自体の削減、③民間・オルタナティブデータによる調査
の代替、という三段階で整理した。これらの効果を実現するには、以下の課題を乗り越える必
要がある。重要なのは、各段階にそれぞれ対応する壁が存在し、電子化から削減、代替へと進
んで効果が大きくなるほど、制度・技術・組織面で乗り越えるべき壁も高くなることである
（図表1）。

図表 1 「効果」の段階と対応する主な「課題」

（出所）大和総研作成

具体的には、以下のような壁が存在する。 
制度・法：行政記録情報や企業保有データを統計に利用する際には、統計法・個別法令上の
利用目的や提供範囲、守秘義務、アクセス手続を整理する必要がある。マイナンバー・法人番
号についても、それぞれの制度上認められた利用範囲を踏まえた手当てが要る。 
標準化・相互運用性：調査ごとに異なる定義・コードの不統一、企業内データと統計上の定
義との粒度ギャップが、データ連携の障害となる。 
府省の縦割り・共通基盤：調査が府省ごとに個別設計され、重複が生じやすい。事業所母集
団データベースは、統計調査結果や行政記録情報を統合し、年次フレームを各府省に提供する
共通基盤として既に運用されている。しかし、重複報告の解消に向けては、収録情報の拡充、
更新頻度の向上、各府省による一層の利活用が課題となる。 
代表性・継続性：民間・オルタナティブデータは統計作成を目的とせずに収集され、サンプ
ルの偏りが生じうる。民間依存ゆえ公表が突然停止されるリスクもある。さらに、政府側も実
証事業として民間・オルタナティブデータを活用した 公的統計を公表している場合、予算等の
関係で公表が停止することもある。例えば、経済産業省のBigData-STATS は2022 年4 月を最後
に更新されておらず、米国商務省経済分析局（BEA）のNear Real-Time Spendingも予算制約に
より 2024 年5 月7 日を最後に更新を終了した。 
品質を担保する仕組み：公的統計には、統計法に加え、公的統計の品質保証に関する政府横

1 溝端幹雄［2026］「なぜ企業は同じ情報を何度も提出しなければならないのか：公的統計のデジタル化を問
う①－負担軽減の『効果』」、大和総研レポート（2026 年10 月16 日） 
「効果」の段階 主な「課題」 手当ての方向
① 電子化 利用率の低迷、システム・様式の不統一 共通認証の徹底、様式標準化、レスポンシブ対応
② 削減 統計法・個別法令上の利用条件、府省間の縦割り、共通基盤の連携不足 利用制度の整備、ビジネスレジスターの高度化
③ 代替 民間・オルタナティブデータの代表性・継続性、共通的な品質評価手順の未整備 公的統計との補完設計、品質ルール・PETs

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

断的な指針や統計委員会による審議・評価の枠組みがある。一方、民間データを公的統計に利
用する場合には、提供主体、対象範囲、生成過程、継続性などがデータごとに異なるため、代
表性や安定性を評価する共通の基準、検証手順、メタデータの整備を一層進める必要がある。 
コストとインセンティブ：企業側のシステム対応・データ整備にはコストがかかる。負担を
求める一方でメリットの還元がなければ、協力は続かない。 
機密・セキュリティ・信頼：企業機密や情報漏洩への懸念に加え、過去の基幹統計調査をめ
ぐる信頼の毀損もあり、安全性と信頼の確保が前提となる。 
人材・予算：統計人材の確保やデジタル投資の財源が不足しがちで、予算制約が根底にある 。

ガバナンス改革：誰が旗を振るか 
上記の課題の多くは、個別の対策だけでは解決が難しく、統計を作る体制そのものに関わる。
前編でも指摘したように、日本は各府省が所管分野の統計を作成する分散型統計機構を採用し
ている。所管分野の専門性を活かせる長所がある一方、様式・定義の標準化や重複排除、府省
共通基盤の整備といった横断的な取組みでは 課題が残りやすい。そのため、総務省による審
査・調整や統計委員会による審議・勧告を、各府省の実施に確実につなげる仕組みが重要とな
る。once-only 原則や共通基盤の整備は、まさにこの横断領域にあたる。 
2018 年の統計法改正（統計法及び独立行政法人統計センター法の一部を改正する法律、平成
30 年法律第 34 号）では、統計委員会に、「統計及び統計制度の発達（筆者注：社会経済の変化
や利用者ニーズに応じて、統計と統計制度を新たに整備・充実・高度化していくこと）及び改
善」に関する基本的事項の調査審議や、基本計画の実施状況に関する勧告などの権限が追加さ
れ、府省横断の調整機能が強化された。しかし、共通基盤の整備や重複報告の解消を実行に移
す上では、なお調整力や実施体制に課題が残る。諸外国の統計機構は大きく「集権型 」「分散
型」に分けることができるが（図表 2）、両者は明確な形で分かれているわけではなく、さらに
「分散型」にも中央機関による基準設定や府省横断調整が強い国（英国や米国） 、各作成主体の
自律性が相対的に強い国（日本）がある。このため、制度類型だけで実行力の差を説明するこ
とは難しい。重要なのは、各国の制度配置そのものよりも、標準化、データアクセス、予算・
人材配分を横断的に調整し、実施を担保する権限と仕組みである。 
現行の第 IV 期の公的統計基本計画（以下、基本計画）の最終年度である 2027 年度を見据え、
次期の第 V 期基本計画では、統計委員会の権限・体制の強化や、府省横断でデータ整備を統括
する機能（諸外国のChief Statistician的な役割）の在り方を、正面から論点に据えるべきで
ある。技術や制度の議論に先立って「誰が旗を振るのか」を定めることが、実装の前提となる。

（注）各国の統計機構は明確な形で分散型・集権型に分かれているわけではない。 さらに、分散型にも中央機
関による基準設定や府省横断調整が強い国 （英国や米国） 、各作成主体の自律性が相対的に強い国（日本）が
ある。 
（出所）大和総研作成

諸外国の先行事例に学ぶ 
日本が向かうべき方向は、諸外国の取組みに具体的な手がかりがある。共通するのは、行政
記録情報や民間データの利用を「その都度の交渉」ではなく「制度」として位置づけ、企業に
同じ情報を二度書かせない仕組み（once-only 原則）を整えている点である（図表3） 。

図表 3 報告者負担軽減に関する諸外国の先行事例

（出所）各国統計機関・欧州委員会資料より大和総研作成

行政記録情報の統計利用（英国） ：英国国家統計局（ONS）は、2017 年 12 月公表の四半期国
民経済計算から、VAT 申告に基づく売上高データを GDP の生産側推計の一部に取り入れた。こ
れは月次企業調査を全面的に代替するものではなく、主に中小企業のデータを補完し、調査デ
ータと組み合わせて利用する仕組みである。さらに、景気変化を早期に捉える Faster 
indicators の取組みでは、VAT 申告に基づく月次指標を研究成果として公表した。ただし、こ
の指標は公式の GDP 統計を代替・予測するものではない。企業に追加の調査票を課さず、既存
の申告データを統計作成に活用する事例として位置づけられる。 
標準ビジネスレポーティング（オランダ・豪州） ：報告項目の定義とデータ様式を標準化し、
企業の会計・業務システムに記録された情報を、複数の行政報告に再利用しやすくする標準ビ
ジネスレポーティング（Standard Business Reporting： SBR）を進めている。会計システムか
ら標準様式で提出できれば、報告ごとの転記や再加工の負担を減らせる。 
論点
分散型
（日本、米国、英国 等）
〔中央調整機能の強さには国ごとに差〕
集権型
（カナダ、豪州、オランダ 等）
統計の作成主体 各府省が所管分野を個別に作成 中央統計局が主要統計を中心的に作成
標準化・重複排除 府省間調整を要し、中央調整機能が弱い場合には推進が難しい 中央が主導しやすい
長所／短所 専門性が高い／横断連携が弱い 連携・効率が高い／専門の分散に留意
国・地域 取組 報告者負担の観点での含意
英国 VAT申告データをGDP推計の一部に導入（2017年～）。2019年から研究系列の月次Faster indicatorsも公表 行政記録利用で「そもそも聞かない」
オランダ・豪州 標準ビジネスレポーティング（SBR） 一度の提出を複数報告で再利用
北欧（デンマーク等） 登録簿（レジスター）で国勢調査を代替 調査票なしで抜本的に軽減
エストニア・EU 行政記録の利用根拠を整備。EUでは対象行政手続にonce-only原則を導入 対象手続で既提出情報の再提出を抑制

### Page 5

1

5 / 9

レジスター型統計（北欧） ：行政記録を統合した登録簿（レジスター）を統計の基盤に据え、
デンマークなどは国勢調査を調査票なしにレジスターで代替している。報告者負担の抜本的な
軽減と統計作成の効率化を両立させた到達点である。 
制度的な取得根拠（エストニア・ EU）：エストニアでは、公式統計法に基づいて統計作成者
が行政記録情報等を利用する仕組みが設けられている。また、行政全体では、法制度、電子ID、
官民のデータベースを安全に接続する X-Road®2等を基盤として、同じ情報を複数の行政機関に
繰り返し提出させない once-only 原則が実装されている。EU でも、単一デジタルゲートウェイ
（Single Digital Gateway：SDG ）3規則などを通じて、対象となる行政手続におけるonce-only
原則の実装が進められている。 
これらに照らすと、日本は行政記録情報の統計利用や府省共通基盤の整備がなお途上にある。
各国が「制度」で担保していることを、日本も次期第 V 期基本計画で制度に落とし込めるかが
問われる。

機密・信頼を「技術」で担保する：PETs 
企業機密や情報漏洩への懸念は、データ提供・連携をためらわせる大きな壁 の一つである。
とりわけ行政記録や民間データを統計に利活用しようとすれば、 「自社の詳細な情報が、目的
外に使われたり漏れたりしないか」という懸念に必ず突き当たる。この懸念は、守秘義務や罰
則といった制度だけでなく、最近では「技術」によっても軽減できるようになってきた。 
その中核が、プライバシー強化技術（Privacy-Enhancing Technologies：PETs）である。代
表例として、複数の当事者が生データを互いに共有せずに計算する「秘密計算（安全な多者間
計算等） 」 、暗号化したまま計算する「準同型暗号」 、集計結果等から個人・個票に関する情報
が推測されるリスクを数理的に制御する「差分プライバシー」 、実データの統計的性質を一定
程度再現した擬似データを用いる「合成データ」がある（ 図表 4） 。国連公的統計ビッグデー
タ・データサイエンス専門家委員会（UN Committee of Experts on Big Data and Data Science 
for Official Statistics：UN- CEBD）は、2023 年に公的統計向けの PETs ガイドを公表してお
り 4、各国統計機関等による実証や実装事例も蓄積されている。これらは、 「データを集めて一
か所に置く」従来の方式に加え、データを移動・開示せずに必要な計算結果を得る選択肢を広
げる。 
日本でも、行政記録情報や民間データを統計に利活用する際に、 「制度（守秘・罰則） 」と
「技術（PETs） 」を両輪として安全性と信頼を確保することが、企業の協力を得る前提となる。

2 e-Estonia, “X-Road”. 
3 EU 域内で国境を越えて生活・事業を行う市民や企業に対して、各国の行政情報・オンライン行政手続・支援
サービスへの共通の入口を提供する制度。 
4 United Nations [2023], United Nations Guide on Privacy-Enhancing Technologies for Official Statistics, United 
Nations Committee of Experts on Big Data and Data Science for Official Statistics, New York.

技術で守れる範囲を広げることは、そのまま「安心して出せる」範囲を広げることにつながる。

図表 4 主なプライバシー強化技術（PETs）の類型

（出所）United Nations[2023], United Nations Guide on Privacy-Enhancing Technologies for Official Statistics よ
り大和総研作成

官民データ供給の制度・経済設計 
民間データの代表性・継続性のリスクは、 「どのように安定して供給を受けるか」の制度・
経済設計によって緩和できる。民間データは統計作成を目的に集められておらず、提供主体の
事業判断によって公表・提供が止まりうる。さらに、政府が実証事業として民間・オルタナテ
ィブデータを活用した公的統計を公表している場合、先述した日本や米国の例のように、政府
の予算制約によってその実証事業が中止されることもある。民間データが安定 的に供給される
には、次の三つの手段を組み合わせる必要がある。 
・法的な提供根拠 ― 統計上の必要性、公益性、代替手段の有無、企業負担、営業秘密の保
護等を踏まえ、対象データと利用条件を限定した提供根拠を整備する。 
・対価・インセンティブ ― 提供に見合う便益や費用補填を用意し、継続的な協力を促す。 
・標準的な提供手続・品質基準 ― データの様式・品質を揃え、継続的な受領と一定の品質
担保を可能にする。 
海外では、民間データへのアクセスや共有を支える枠組みの整備が進む。欧州データ法
（Data Act ）は、コネクテッド製品 5や関連サービスから生じるデータへのアクセスや利用に関
する横断的なルールを定めるとともに、例外的な必要性がある場合に公的機関が民間保有デー
タを求める仕組みを設けた6。また、欧州共通データスペース（Common European Data Spaces）
は、分野別のデータ共有基盤や相互運用性の整備を進めている 7。もっとも、これらは公的統
計への民間データの恒常的な供給を一般的に義務づける制度ではない。日本でも、統計目的で
民間データを継続利用する場合の根拠、手続、費用負担、品質管理を個別に制度設計する必要
がある（図表5）。

5 自動車の走行・故障データ、機械の稼働・保守データ、スマート家電の利用状況データなど、IoT 製品。 
6 European Commission, “Data Act explained”。根拠条文は規則（EU）2023/2854 の第2 章・第5 章（特に
第 14～15 条）（2025 年 12 月15 日最終更新）。 
7 European Commission, “Common European data spaces”。 
技術 概要 主な用途
秘密計算（安全な多者間計算等） 生データを共有せず統計値のみ算出 府省・官民をまたぐ集計
準同型暗号 暗号化したまま計算 機密データの安全な処理
差分プライバシー 集計結果等から個人・個票に関する情報が推測されるリスクを数学的に制御 統計の安全な公表
合成データ 実データの統計的性質を一定程度再現した擬似データを生成 分析・研究、システム開発、安全性を評価した上での外部提供
